ledger-source.jsx

Ledger app source code. For the project knowledge area. Hosted as a PWA on GitHub Pages. Latest build: suggestCardioTarget was rebuilt against the running-science literature. It no longer anchors on the best run in the window — that ratcheted, because one unusually good day raised the floor permanently and ordinary variation read as a new sustainable baseline. The anchor is now the median distance across the window, which shrugs off a single outlier in either direction. Distance no longer advances because pace improved; advancing volume and intensity together is the one combination endurance practice is unanimous about separating, so a faster pace over the same ground is now reported as banked fitness and the target holds. Progression comes instead from consistency: four or more runs in the window with the last three at or above the anchor earns a 5% step. The old 5% heart-rate-drift hold fired on a signal that moves with heat, sleep, caffeine and how hard the lifting was beforehand; a hold now requires pace and heart rate to agree — slower and higher together. Not yet done, and both need data the app doesn't collect: weekly minutes as the primary progression axis rather than per-session distance, and session-RPE as the cross-surface load measure. RunGraph sits at the top of the Progression wall on Today, plotting every run ever logged with no cap — dot radius scales with spacing so density degrades instead of history being dropped. Distance is drawn as dots rather than a step line, because run distance moves every session and the ladder's step-after path would be a jagged line wearing a staircase costume; what steps is the target, so the target gets the ember step line, replayed through suggestCardioTarget on sessions.slice(0, run.index) so it can never disagree with the plan the coach shows. timeConstrained runs render as hollow muted dots, visible but outside the targeting maths. Pace sits in its own band below a hairline rule, inverted so faster is higher. cardioGroup() folds treadmill and outdoor into one running group (pool stays separate on yards); a surface anchors on its own history at two or more runs and borrows from the whole running group below that, so a first outdoor run isn't told to build a 1.5mi baseline and a long outdoor run never sets the treadmill target. Previously: the History tab is editable via SessionEditor, which holds a local draft and writes once on Save; handleSessionUpdate re-sorts sessions by date afterwards because every reader of history walks the array in order. The teachings importer rewrites suttacentral.net links to its JSON text API and gates imports on TEACHING_MIN_CHARS (1000). Session type “R” (run-only) skips movement prep and lifting and stays out of the A/B alternation. Stretches are capped at STRETCH_SESSION_CAP (5). hipThrust uses the loaded bench version via loadFloor (25lb) through migrateExercises.

// Lightweight inline icons (standalone build has no lucide-react dependency)
const Icon = ({ children, size = 16, color = "currentColor", style }) => (
  <svg width={size} height={size} viewBox="0 0 24 24" fill="none" stroke={color} strokeWidth="2" strokeLinecap="round" strokeLinejoin="round" style={style}>{children}</svg>
);
const ChevronRight = (p) => <Icon {...p}><polyline points="9 18 15 12 9 6" /></Icon>;
const ChevronLeft = (p) => <Icon {...p}><polyline points="15 18 9 12 15 6" /></Icon>;

const Pencil = (p) => <Icon {...p}><path d="M12 20h9" /><path d="M16.5 3.5a2.12 2.12 0 013 3L7 19l-4 1 1-4z" /></Icon>;

// Written once and shared by the seed config, the library entry and the
// migration, so the three can't drift apart the way the old floor-first
// version did once the exercise had already been saved to storage.
const HIP_THRUST_EXPLAINER = "Set up on a bench: sit on the floor with your upper back against the long edge of a bench, knees bent, feet flat and about shoulder width. Roll a dumbbell over your hips and rest it in the crease, with a pad or folded towel underneath it. Drive through your heels until your body forms a straight line from knees to shoulders, squeeze the glutes hard for a second at the top, then lower under control.\n\nForm cues: the squeeze comes from the glutes, not from arching your low back — stop when your hips are level with knees and shoulders, not higher. Chin slightly tucked, ribs down. If you feel it in your low back rather than your glutes, your feet are probably too far away; walk them closer. If the bar of the dumbbell digs in, more padding rather than less weight.\n\nThe floor version is the same movement without the bench, and is worth one set as a warm-up. It is not worth programming on its own — unloaded, it sits below the load anything adapts to.";

// Stretch programming follows the accumulated-time-per-muscle-group model:
// research consistently finds ~60s total per muscle group per session is the
// effective dose, and that 30s holds are as good as longer ones (benefit
// largely plateaus past 60s in a single hold). So progression happens by
// adding ROUNDS toward a ~60s accumulated target, not by holding longer.
// `emphasis` marks which session a stretch is most relevant to — static
// stretching is most useful on the muscles you actually just trained.

// Fresh-start snapshot as of the 2026-08-03 export. Only consulted when
// localStorage is empty; stored data always wins. Deliberately NOT folded into
// DEFAULT_EXERCISES - mergeExercises() re-applies every DEFAULT key on load, so a
// custom lift living there could never be deleted.
const SEED_EXERCISES = {

    ],

    "estMinutes": 7,

    "addedAt": "2026-07-31"
  },
};

// Movement patterns are how trainers check a program for holes. A balanced
// routine trains each of these; a gap means a whole muscle group or quality
// is going untrained no matter how hard you work the others.
const PATTERNS = {
  horizontalPush: "Horizontal push (chest, front shoulder, triceps)",

  horizontalPull: "Horizontal pull (mid-back, rear shoulder)",

  kneeDominant: "Knee-dominant legs (quads)",

// Candidate movements ordered by how much the missing pattern costs. Free
// weights preferred where they train the pattern better than a machine.
// estMinutes feeds the session time budget. Explainers are written in, so
// nothing here needs an API key.
const EXERCISE_LIBRARY = [
  {
    key: "hipThrust", label: "Hip Thrust (bench)", unit: "lb", targetSets: 3, targetReps: 12, step: 10,

    why: "The biggest hole in your program. Leg press trains the front of the leg; nothing trains the back — glutes and hamstrings. This is the version of that work that's safest to learn on your own: your back is supported throughout, and if the weight is too heavy you simply can't lift it, rather than being pulled into a bad position under load. Loaded from the start — unloaded on the floor it is a warm-up, not a working set.",

    why: "Chest press covers pressing forward, but nothing presses overhead. That leaves the shoulder trained in one direction only — the most common gap in a machine-based program.",
    explainer: "Standing or seated with back support, hold dumbbells at shoulder height, palms facing forward. Press straight up until your arms are extended but not locked hard, then lower under control back to shoulder height.\n\nForm cues: keep your ribs down — don't arch your low back to get the weight up. Squeeze your glutes if standing; it stops the lean automatically. Wrists stacked over elbows, not bent back. const uid = () => Date.now() + Math.random();
// Local calendar date (not UTC) — toISOString() rolls to the next day in the
// evening for US timezones, which would misdate anything logged after ~7-8pm.
const localDateStr = (d = new Date()) => {
  const y = d.getFullYear(), m = String(d.getMonth() + 1).padStart(2, "0"), day = String(d.getDate()).padStart(2, "0");
  return `${y}-${m}-${day}`;
};
// A session's stored .type is only reliable once this fix shipped — earlier
// saved sessions all got the literal "logged" regardless of what was actually
// done. Infer A/B from which exercises are present so old data (and the
// alternation logic that depends on it) still works.
function effectiveType(s, exercises) {
  // "R" is a run-only day: cardio, optional bodyweight work, no lifting. It is a
  // real logged session, but it is deliberately never inferred from exercises and
  // never stands in for A or B, so it can't satisfy the alternation check or be
  // read as a missed strength session.
  if (s.type === "A" || s.type === "B" || s.type === "R") return s.type;
  const keys = Object.keys(s.exercises || {});
  if (keys.length === 0) return s.type;
  const types = keys.map((k) => exercises[k]?.sessionType).filter(Boolean);
  const aCount = types.filter((t) => t === "A").length;
  const bCount = types.filter((t) => t === "B").length;
  if (aCount > bCount) return "A";
  if (bCount > aCount) return "B";
  return s.type;
}

// ---------- storage helpers ----------
function lsGet(key) {
  try {
    const v = localStorage.getItem("ledger:" + key);
    return v === null ? null : { value: v };
  } catch (e) { return null; }
}
// Human names for the storage keys, so a refused write can say which thing
// didn't stick instead of "something went wrong".
const STORAGE_LABELS = {
  "sessions": "that session",
  "plan": "that plan change",
  "practice-entries": "that practice entry",
  "exercises-config": "that exercise change",
  "stretches-config": "that stretch change",
  "recipes-config": "that recipe",
  "meal-plan": "that meal plan change",
  "sila": "that sila marker",
  "kitchen-rules": "that kitchen rule",
  "log-draft": "the in-progress workout draft",
  "teachings": "that teaching",
};

// A refused write is silent by default. setItem throws when the quota is full,
// but it can also accept the call and store nothing, which is how a change can
// sit on screen all evening and be gone in the morning. Kitchen used to be the
// only place that checked, with its own timer at the one call site. The check
// lives inside the write now, so every entry type gets it without having to
// remember to ask for it.
let storageListener = null;
function onStorageFailure(fn) {
  storageListener = fn;
  return () => { if (storageListener === fn) storageListener = null; };
}
function reportStorageFailure(key) {
  const what = STORAGE_LABELS[key] || "that change";
  // Deferred: several saves run inside a setState updater, and a React state
  // setter must never fire synchronously from inside one.
  setTimeout(() => { if (storageListener) storageListener(what); }, 0);
}

function lsSet(key, value) {
  try {
    localStorage.setItem("ledger:" + key, value);
    // Read it straight back. If what comes out isn't what went in, the change
    // exists only in memory and will not survive a restart.
    if (localStorage.getItem("ledger:" + key) !== value) {
      reportStorageFailure(key);
      return false;
    }
    return true;
  } catch (e) {
    reportStorageFailure(key);
    return false;
  }
}
function lsRemove(key) {
  try { localStorage.removeItem("ledger:" + key); return true; } catch (e) { return false; }
}

// ---------- in-progress workout draft ----------
// The log wizard holds a whole session in memory before it's committed, so
// closing the tab mid-workout used to lose everything typed so far. The draft
// is written on every change and restored on next load, which makes an
// accidental tab close (or a phone killing the tab in the background) a
// non-event: reopen the file and the Resume banner picks it up where it was.
const DRAFT_KEY = "log-draft";
const DRAFT_MAX_AGE_MS = 7 * 24 * 60 * 60 * 1000;

function saveDraft(draft) {
  lsSet(DRAFT_KEY, JSON.stringify(draft));
}
function clearDraft() {
  lsRemove(DRAFT_KEY);
}
function loadDraft() {
  try {
    const raw = lsGet(DRAFT_KEY);
    if (!raw) return null;
    const d = JSON.parse(raw.value);
    if (!d || !d.entry) return null;
    // Don't nag about a workout abandoned weeks ago.
    if (d.savedAt && Date.now() - d.savedAt > DRAFT_MAX_AGE_MS) {
      clearDraft();
      return null;
    }
    return d;
  } catch (e) { return null; }
}
function draftSummary(d) {
  const n = Object.keys((d.entry && d.entry.exercises) || {}).length;
  const when = d.savedAt ? new Date(d.savedAt) : null;
  const day = when ? (localDateStr(when) === localDateStr() ? "today" : localDateStr(when)) : "";
  const time = when ? when.toLocaleTimeString([], { hour: "numeric", minute: "2-digit" }) : "";
  const type = d.entry && d.entry.type ? `Session ${d.entry.type}` : "Session";
  const stamp = day ? `${day}${time ? ", " + time : ""}` : "";
  return `${type}${stamp ? " — started " + stamp : ""}. ${n} exercise${n === 1 ? "" : "s"} entered so far.`;
}

// Guidance text (progressionRule, restGuidance) is app-authored, not
// user-edited — always take the current version so fixes/updates actually
// reach someone who already has a plan saved from an earlier version.
// goals/queue are real user state and are preserved from storage.
function mergePlan(stored) {
  if (!stored) return DEFAULT_PLAN;
  return {
    ...DEFAULT_PLAN,
    ...stored,
    progressionRule: DEFAULT_PLAN.progressionRule,
    restGuidance: DEFAULT_PLAN.restGuidance,
    stretchGuidance: DEFAULT_PLAN.stretchGuidance,
  };
}
// Backfills new default fields (e.g. sessionType) onto exercises a user
// already has saved from before that field existed, without clobbering
// their own customizations (active status, custom exercises, etc.).
// Stretch config is a list, not a map. Merge by key so base stretches pick up
// any changed defaults while user-added library stretches are preserved.
// Recipes are user-edited (outcome, lastMade) but their reference data is
// app-authored, so keep the user's fields and refresh the rest.
function mergeRecipes(stored) {
  if (!Array.isArray(stored) || stored.length === 0) return DEFAULT_RECIPES;
  const byKey = new Map(stored.map((r) => [r.key, r]));
  const merged = DEFAULT_RECIPES.map((base) => {
    const u = byKey.get(base.key);
    if (!u) return base;
    // A blank stored outcome falls back to the app-authored note rather than
    // shadowing it, so clearing the box resets to the original note instead of
    // leaving the card silent. Everything the cook records — verdicts most of
    // all, since the chef reads them — has to survive the merge, or a "never
    // again" quietly reverts the next time the app opens.
    return {
      ...base,
      outcome: (u.outcome || "").trim() ? u.outcome : base.outcome,
      lastMade: u.lastMade ?? null,
      verdict: u.verdict ?? null,
      verdictNote: u.verdictNote || "",
      cuisine: u.cuisine || base.cuisine || "",
      // Edits and provenance are pure cook-authored data with no app-authored
      // original to fall back on. Same class of bug as the verdict one: leave
      // them out of the merge and they vanish at the next launch.
      edits: Array.isArray(u.edits) ? u.edits : (base.edits || []),
      source: u.source || base.source || null,
    };
  });
  const baseKeys = new Set(DEFAULT_RECIPES.map((r) => r.key));
  stored.forEach((r) => { if (!baseKeys.has(r.key)) merged.push(r); });
  return merged;
}
function mergeStretches(stored) {
  if (!Array.isArray(stored) || stored.length === 0) return STRETCHES;
  const byKey = new Map(stored.map((s) => [s.key, s]));
  const merged = STRETCHES.map((base) => ({ ...base, ...(byKey.get(base.key) || {}) }));
  const baseKeys = new Set(STRETCHES.map((s) => s.key));
  stored.forEach((s) => { if (!baseKeys.has(s.key)) merged.push(s); });
  return merged;
}
function mergeExercises(stored) {
  if (!stored) return DEFAULT_EXERCISES;
  const merged = { ...stored };
  for (const key of Object.keys(DEFAULT_EXERCISES)) {
    merged[key] = { ...DEFAULT_EXERCISES[key], ...(stored[key] || {}) };
  }
  return merged;
}

// Stored config always wins over the defaults, which is right for weights and
// rep targets and wrong for everything else: an exercise added from the library
// months ago keeps whatever text and fields it had the day it was added, so a
// corrected explainer or a new field never reaches it. Each migration is gated
// on the field it introduces, so it applies once and then stops.
function migrateExercises(exercises) {
  const out = { ...exercises };
  const ht = out.hipThrust;
  if (ht && ht.loadFloor == null) {
    out.hipThrust = { ...ht, label: "Hip Thrust (bench)", loadFloor: 25, explainer: HIP_THRUST_EXPLAINER };
  }
  return out;
}

async function loadState() {
  try {
    const s = lsGet("sessions");
    const p = lsGet("plan");
    let practice = [];
    const pr = lsGet("practice-entries");
    practice = pr ? JSON.parse(pr.value) : SEED_PRACTICE;
    let exercises = DEFAULT_EXERCISES;
    const ex = lsGet("exercises-config");
    exercises = migrateExercises(ex ? mergeExercises(JSON.parse(ex.value)) : SEED_EXERCISES);
    const st = lsGet("stretches-config");
    const stretches = st ? mergeStretches(JSON.parse(st.value)) : STRETCHES;
    const rc = lsGet("recipes-config");
    const recipes = rc ? mergeRecipes(JSON.parse(rc.value)) : DEFAULT_RECIPES;
    const mp = lsGet("meal-plan");
    const mealPlan = mp ? JSON.parse(mp.value) : SEED_MEAL_PLAN;
    const sl = lsGet("sila");
    const sila = sl ? JSON.parse(sl.value) : {};
    // Parsed on its own rather than inside the shared try: a malformed
    // teachings blob should cost the teachings, not send every other section
    // back to its seed data for the rest of the session.
    let teachings = [];
    try {
      const tg = lsGet(TEACHINGS_KEY);
      const parsed = tg ? JSON.parse(tg.value) : [];
      if (Array.isArray(parsed)) teachings = parsed;
    } catch (e) {}
    const kr = lsGet("kitchen-rules");
    const kitchenRules = kr ? mergeRules(JSON.parse(kr.value)) : SEED_RULES;
    return {
      sila,
      kitchenRules,
      teachings,
      sessions: s ? JSON.parse(s.value) : SEED_SESSIONS,
      plan: p ? mergePlan(JSON.parse(p.value)) : null,
      practice,
      exercises,
      stretches,
      recipes,
      mealPlan,
    };
  } catch (e) {
    return { sessions: SEED_SESSIONS, plan: null, practice: SEED_PRACTICE, exercises: SEED_EXERCISES, stretches: STRETCHES, recipes: DEFAULT_RECIPES, mealPlan: SEED_MEAL_PLAN, sila: {}, kitchenRules: SEED_RULES, teachings: [] };
  }
}
async function saveSessions(sessions) { lsSet("sessions", JSON.stringify(sessions)); }
async function savePlan(plan) { lsSet("plan", JSON.stringify(plan)); }
async function savePractice(entries) { lsSet("practice-entries", JSON.stringify(entries)); }
async function saveExercises(exercises) { lsSet("exercises-config", JSON.stringify(exercises)); }
async function saveStretches(stretches) { lsSet("stretches-config", JSON.stringify(stretches)); }
async function saveRecipes(recipes) { lsSet("recipes-config", JSON.stringify(recipes)); }
async function saveMealPlan(mealPlan) { lsSet("meal-plan", JSON.stringify(mealPlan)); }
async function saveSila(sila) { lsSet("sila", JSON.stringify(sila)); }
async function saveKitchenRules(rules) { lsSet("kitchen-rules", JSON.stringify(rules)); }
async function saveTeachings(teachings) { lsSet(TEACHINGS_KEY, JSON.stringify(teachings)); }

function getApiKey() {
  try { return localStorage.getItem("ledger:api-key") || ""; } catch (e) { return ""; }
}
function setApiKey(key) {
  try { localStorage.setItem("ledger:api-key", key); } catch (e) {}
}

async function askClaude(prompt) {
  const apiKey = getApiKey();
  if (!apiKey) throw new Error("NO_API_KEY");
  const response = await fetch("https://api.anthropic.com/v1/messages", {
    method: "POST",
    headers: {
      "Content-Type": "application/json",
      "x-api-key": apiKey,
      "anthropic-version": "2023-06-01",
      "anthropic-dangerous-direct-browser-access": "true",
    },
    body: JSON.stringify({
      model: "claude-sonnet-4-6",
      max_tokens: 800,
      messages: [{ role: "user", content: prompt }],
    }),
  });
  if (!response.ok) {
    const errText = await response.text();
    throw new Error("API error: " + response.status + " " + errText.slice(0, 200));
  }
  const data = await response.json();
  const text = (data.content || [])
    .filter((c) => c.type === "text")
    .map((c) => c.text)
    .join("\n");
  return text || "No response.";
}

async function generateExplainer(name) {
  const prompt = `Give a short, practical how-to guide for the gym exercise "${name}", written for someone learning it for the first time at a commercial gym. Cover: (1) setup/starting position, (2) the movement itself in 3-5 steps, (3) the 2-3 most important form cues to avoid injury or wasted effort. Keep it under 180 words, plain language, no headers, just tight prose or a short list.`;
  return askClaude(prompt);
}

const DEFAULT_PLAN = {
  goals: ["Flexibility", "Strength", "Physique"],
  progressionRule: "Double progression: reps increase first (up to +4 over target) across two easy sessions, then weight steps up and reps reset. A struggled session resets to the last stable weight. Cardio target adjusts from recent pace/HR trend. An exercise unchanged for 4 sessions with a struggle gets flagged for a deload or swap.",
  restGuidance: "Aim for at least one full rest day (no structured exercise) somewhere in most 7-day stretches. Session A and B alternate muscle emphasis, so back-to-back A/B days are fine — but a full day off matters too, for tissue recovery and for the stress-reduction goal specifically.",
  sessionBudgetMinutes: 120,
  stretchGuidance: "Dynamic movement prep before lifting; static stretching after, while warm. Static holds add up per muscle group — roughly 60s total is the effective dose, and progression happens by adding rounds rather than holding longer, since benefit largely plateaus past that. Stretches are split by session so you're stretching what you actually trained. Flexibility is the goal most driven by frequency: 2-3 days a week is the floor, and the routine works at home on non-gym days.",
  queue: [

    { key: "legPress", note: "95lb rated easy — bump to 105–110lb next attempt." },

  ],
};

// Looks at the trailing 7 calendar days and flags when there hasn't been a
// genuine day off — distinct from muscle-group alternation (which A/B
// already handles), this is about whole-system recovery.
function assessRestPattern(sessions) {
  const today = new Date();
  const loggedDates = new Set(sessions.map((s) => s.date));
  let trainedDays = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < 7; i++) {
    const d = new Date(today);
    d.setDate(d.getDate() - i);
    if (loggedDates.has(localDateStr(d))) trainedDays++;
  }
  const restDays = 7 - trainedDays;

  const sortedDates = [...loggedDates].sort();
  const lastDate = sortedDates[sortedDates.length - 1];
  let daysSinceLast = null;
  if (lastDate) {
    const [y, m, d] = lastDate.split("-").map(Number);
    const last = new Date(y, m - 1, d);
    const todayMidnight = new Date();
    todayMidnight.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);
    daysSinceLast = Math.round((todayMidnight - last) / 86400000);
  }

  let flag = null;
  if (restDays === 0) {
    flag = "No full rest day in the last 7 days. A/B alternation spreads out muscle-group load, but whole-system recovery still benefits from at least one genuine day off — consider taking tomorrow easy.";
  } else if (restDays === 1) {
    flag = "Only one rest day in the last 7 — fine occasionally, but worth keeping an eye on if it becomes the norm.";
  }

  return { restDaysLast7: restDays, daysSinceLast, flag };
}

// Looks at the most recent logged sessions and flags when things have been
// consistently going well — a reasonable signal that it's a good time to
// consider adding a new exercise from the chamber.
function assessChamberReadiness(sessions) {
  const withExercises = sessions.filter((s) => s.exercises && Object.keys(s.exercises).length > 0);
  const recent = withExercises.slice(-3);
  if (recent.length < 3) {
    return { ready: false, reason: `Log ${3 - recent.length} more session${3 - recent.length === 1 ? "" : "s"} with exercises before a readiness check makes sense.` };
  }
  let struggled = 0, total = 0;
  recent.forEach((s) => {
    Object.values(s.exercises).forEach((v) => {
      total += 1;
      if (v.feel === "struggled" || v.feel === "hard") struggled += 1;
    });
  });
  if (total === 0) return { ready: false, reason: "" };
  const struggleRate = struggled / total;
  if (struggleRate === 0) {
    return { ready: true, reason: "Your last 3 sessions were all solid or easy — no struggling exercises. Good sign it's a fine time to add something new." };
  }
  if (struggleRate <= 0.25) {
    return { ready: false, reason: "Mostly steady lately, but a recent struggle or two — might be worth one more consistent session before adding load." };
  }
  return { ready: false, reason: "A few exercises have been tough recently — better to let things settle before adding something new." };
}

// ---------- stretch engine ----------
// Older sessions stored stretch results as strings ("full"/"shortened"/
// "skipped") with no round count. Normalize both shapes to a round number so
// history stays usable and progression can reason over all of it.
function normalizeStretchRounds(value, cfg) {
  if (value == null) return null;
  if (typeof value === "number") return value;
  if (typeof value === "object" && typeof value.rounds === "number") return value.rounds;
  if (value === "skipped") return 0;
  if (value === "shortened") return Math.max(1, (cfg?.baseRounds ?? 1) - 1);
  if (value === "full") return cfg?.baseRounds ?? 1;
  return null;
}

function stretchAccum(cfg, rounds) {
  return (cfg.holdSeconds || 0) * (rounds || 0);
}

// A stretch counts as fully dosed once it's within reach of the ~60s target.
// Without this band, a 45s hold would "progress" to 90s — well past the point
// where added time stops doing anything, which is the opposite of the intent.
const STRETCH_ENOUGH = STRETCH_TARGET_ACCUM * 0.75;
function stretchAtDose(cfg, rounds) {
  return stretchAccum(cfg, rounds) >= STRETCH_ENOUGH;
}

// Rounds progress only once the current dose is being completed consistently,
// and stop at the ~60s accumulated target — past that the evidence shows
// little added benefit, so more time would just be time spent for nothing.
function getStretchRecommendation(sessions, cfg) {
  const history = sessions
    .map((s) => normalizeStretchRounds((s.stretchRounds || {})[cfg.key], cfg))
    .filter((r) => r != null);

  if (history.length === 0) {
    return {
      rounds: cfg.baseRounds,
      action: "start",
      rationale: `${cfg.holdSeconds}s${cfg.perSide ? " per side" : ""} × ${cfg.baseRounds} round${cfg.baseRounds === 1 ? "" : "s"} — stretch to mild tension, never pain.`,
    };
  }

  const recent = history.slice(-3);
  const lastRounds = history[history.length - 1];
  const consistent = recent.length >= 2 && recent.slice(-2).every((r) => r >= lastRounds && r > 0);

  if (lastRounds === 0) {
    return {
      rounds: cfg.baseRounds,
      action: "resume",
      rationale: `Skipped last time — back to ${cfg.baseRounds} round${cfg.baseRounds === 1 ? "" : "s"} of ${cfg.holdSeconds}s.`,
    };
  }

  if (stretchAtDose(cfg, lastRounds)) {
    return {
      rounds: lastRounds,
      action: "maintain",
      rationale: `${stretchAccum(cfg, lastRounds)}s total — at the effective dose. Holding here; more time adds little.`,
    };
  }

  if (consistent) {
    const nextRounds = lastRounds + 1;
    return {
      rounds: nextRounds,
      action: "rounds-up",
      rationale: `Consistent at ${lastRounds} round${lastRounds === 1 ? "" : "s"} — adding a round (${stretchAccum(cfg, nextRounds)}s total) toward the ~${STRETCH_TARGET_ACCUM}s dose.`,
    };
  }

  return {
    rounds: lastRounds,
    action: "hold",
    rationale: `Repeat ${lastRounds} round${lastRounds === 1 ? "" : "s"} of ${cfg.holdSeconds}s — build consistency before adding volume.`,
  };
}

// Stretches relevant to today's session: everything marked "both", plus the
// ones that emphasize the muscles this session actually trains.
// Shortest holds first, longest last. The point is practical rather than
// physiological: a run of 30s holds can be timed on the watch once and worked
// straight through, instead of resetting it between every stretch. Rounds break
// a tie, so more repetitions also drift later, and Child's Pose at 60s ends up
// where it belongs — at the end.
//
// Sort is stable, so within a duration the stretches keep the order they were
// defined in, and adding one doesn't shuffle the rest.
function byHoldLength(a, b) {
  const hold = (a.holdSeconds || 0) - (b.holdSeconds || 0);
  if (hold !== 0) return hold;
  return (a.baseRounds || 1) - (b.baseRounds || 1);
}

function orderStretches(list) {
  return [...list].sort(byHoldLength);
}

function stretchesForSession(sessionType, allStretches = STRETCHES) {
  return orderStretches(allStretches.filter((s) => s.active !== false && (s.emphasis === "both" || s.emphasis === sessionType)));
}

// The pool and the session are two different things. A wide pool is what gives
// the routine somewhere to draw from and somewhere to put a new stretch; a wide
// session is just ten minutes of holds at the end of a workout, which is the
// part that gets skipped. So the pool keeps growing and the session stays capped.
//
// Three ranges are trained every session because they're the ones being trained
// on purpose — hip flexors and hamstrings for sitting, calves for running.
// Range gains are specific to the muscle stretched, so these only pay off if
// they come round almost every time. The remaining slots rotate: whichever of
// the rest is most overdue, with a nudge toward whatever this session trained,
// since a static stretch does most for a muscle that has just been worked.
const STRETCH_SESSION_CAP = 5;
const STRETCH_ANCHORS = ["hipFlexor", "hamstring", "calf"];

function daysSinceStretched(sessions, key) {
  const done = sessions.filter((s) => normalizeStretchRounds((s.stretchRounds || {})[key], null) > 0);
  if (done.length === 0) return 45;
  const last = done[done.length - 1].date;
  if (!last) return 45;
  const [y, m, d] = last.split("-").map(Number);
  const today = new Date();
  today.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);
  const days = Math.round((today.valueOf() - new Date(y, m - 1, d).valueOf()) / 86400000);
  return Math.max(0, Math.min(45, days));
}

function selectSessionStretches(sessionType, allStretches = STRETCHES, sessions = [], cap = STRETCH_SESSION_CAP) {
  const pool = stretchesForSession(sessionType, allStretches);
  if (pool.length <= cap) return pool;
  const anchors = pool.filter((s) => STRETCH_ANCHORS.indexOf(s.key) !== -1);
  const rest = pool.filter((s) => STRETCH_ANCHORS.indexOf(s.key) === -1);
  const slots = Math.max(0, cap - anchors.length);
  const scored = rest
    .map((s) => ({ s, stale: daysSinceStretched(sessions, s.key), matched: s.emphasis === sessionType ? 6 : 0 }))
    .sort((a, b) => (b.stale + b.matched) - (a.stale + a.matched) || a.s.key.localeCompare(b.s.key));
  return orderStretches(anchors.concat(scored.slice(0, slots).map((x) => x.s)));
}

// Flexibility is the one goal that responds to frequency more than intensity —
// guidelines put the floor at 2-3 days/week and note daily is better for
// actually gaining range. Gym-day-only stretching sits right at that floor.
function assessStretchFrequency(sessions) {
  const today = new Date();
  const stretchedDates = new Set(
    sessions
      .filter((s) => Object.values(s.stretchRounds || {}).some((v) => normalizeStretchRounds(v, null) > 0))
      .map((s) => s.date)
  );
  let days = 0;
  for (let i = 0; i < 7; i++) {
    const d = new Date(today);
    d.setDate(d.getDate() - i);
    if (stretchedDates.has(localDateStr(d))) days++;
  }
  let flag = null;
  if (days > 0 && days < 2) {
    flag = `Stretched ${days} day${days === 1 ? "" : "s"} in the last 7. Flexibility responds to frequency more than any other goal — guidelines put the useful floor at 2-3 days a week. The routine works fine on non-gym days at home.`;
  }
  return { daysLast7: days, flag };
}

// Per-exercise progression can look healthy while a whole session type
// quietly goes untrained — the imbalance a trainer notices and the app
// otherwise would not.
function assessSessionBalance(sessions, exercises) {
  const lifts = sessions.filter((s) => s.exercises && Object.keys(s.exercises).length > 0);
  if (lifts.length < 3) return { flag: null };

  const lastByType = {};
  lifts.forEach((s) => {
    const t = effectiveType(s, exercises);
    if (t === "A" || t === "B") lastByType[t] = s.date;
  });

  const today = new Date();
  today.setHours(0, 0, 0, 0);
  const daysSince = (dateStr) => {
    if (!dateStr) return Infinity;
    const [y, m, d] = dateStr.split("-").map(Number);
    return Math.round((today - new Date(y, m - 1, d)) / 86400000);
  };

  const aDays = daysSince(lastByType.A);
  const bDays = daysSince(lastByType.B);
  const [stale, staleDays, fresh] = aDays > bDays ? ["A", aDays, "B"] : ["B", bDays, "A"];
  if (!isFinite(staleDays) && !isFinite(aDays) && !isFinite(bDays)) return { flag: null };

  if (staleDays >= 7) {
    const label = staleDays === Infinity ? "hasn't been trained yet" : `hasn't had a lifting session in ${staleDays} days`;
    return {
      staleType: stale,
      flag: `Session ${stale} ${label}, while Session ${fresh} has kept moving. Per-exercise progress still looks fine, which is exactly how a whole half of a program drifts out of the rotation. Session ${stale} next.`,
    };
  }
  return { flag: null };
}
